// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#1 — direct children, file logs and bounded cleanup own proof orchestration.
import { spawn, type ChildProcess } from "node:child_process";
import { closeSync, mkdirSync, openSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join, resolve } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { setTimeout as sleep } from "node:timers/promises";
import { stripVTControlCharacters } from "node:util";

const children = new Set<ChildProcess>();
const privateGroups = new WeakMap<ChildProcess, number>();
const groupClosures = new WeakMap<ChildProcess, Promise<void>>();
process.once("exit", () => { for (const child of children) child.kill("SIGKILL"); });
export function register(child: ChildProcess): void {
  children.add(child);
  child.once("exit", () => children.delete(child));
  child.on("error", () => children.delete(child));
}
export interface ProofOptions {
  cwd?: string;
  env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
  args?: readonly string[];
  timeoutMs?: number;
  stopTimeoutMs?: number;
  logsDir?: string;
  /** Keep stdout in memory only (stderr remains diagnostic evidence). */
  secret?: boolean;
  /** Internal: a frontend keeper owns this private process group. */
  detached?: boolean;
  /** Internal: the frontend observes parent death through stdin EOF. */
  livenessPipe?: boolean;
  /** Internal: a keeper-backed group owns npm and other descendant trees. */
  processGroup?: boolean;
}
export interface ProofHost {
  url: string;
  pid: number;
  logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string };
  stop(): Promise<void>;
}
export function deadline(ms: number | undefined, fallback: number): number {
  const value = ms ?? fallback;
  if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value < 1) throw new RangeError("proof timeout must be positive and finite");
  return value;
}
/** @throws deadline expires; work rejection propagates. */
export async function bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T> {
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
  try {
    return await Promise.race([work, new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
      timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`${label} timed out after ${ms} ms`)), ms);
    })]);
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}
/** Attempts every cleanup, even if a preceding step fails or stalls. */
/** @throws AggregateError after every cleanup was attempted; each step has a 10-second bound. */
export async function cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown | Promise<unknown>) | undefined>): Promise<void> {
  const errors: unknown[] = [];
  for (const step of steps) if (step) {
    try { await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(step), 10_000, "proof cleanup"); }
    catch (error) { errors.push(error); }
  }
  if (errors.length) throw new AggregateError(errors, "proof cleanup failed");
}
/** @throws invalid handle/deadline, host stop failure or TERM/KILL reap deadline. */
export async function stopChild(child: ChildProcess | Pick<ProofHost, "url" | "stop">, graceMs = 5000): Promise<void> {
  deadline(graceMs, 5000);
  if (child && "url" in child && typeof child.url === "string" && typeof child.stop === "function") {
    await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(() => child.stop()), graceMs + 10_000, "host stop"); return;
  }
  if (!child || !("kill" in child) || typeof child.kill !== "function" || typeof child.once !== "function"
      || !(child.exitCode === null || typeof child.exitCode === "number")
      || !(child.signalCode === null || typeof child.signalCode === "string")) {
    throw new TypeError("stopChild needs a ChildProcess or { url, stop }");
  }
  if (child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null) { await groupGone(child); return; }
  let done!: () => void;
  const exited = new Promise<void>(resolve => { done = resolve; });
  child.once("exit", done);
  child.once("error", done);
  try {
    child.kill("SIGTERM");
    try { await bounded(exited, graceMs, "SIGTERM"); }
    catch {
      child.kill("SIGKILL");
      await bounded(exited, 5000, "SIGKILL reap");
    }
  } finally { child.removeListener("exit", done); child.removeListener("error", done); }
  await groupGone(child);
}
async function groupGone(child: ChildProcess): Promise<void> {
  const pid = privateGroups.get(child);
  if (!pid || process.platform === "win32") return;
  const existing = groupClosures.get(child);
  if (existing) return existing;
  const cleanup = (async () => {
    const expires = performance.now() + 5000;
    for (;;) {
      try { process.kill(-pid, 0); }
      catch (error) {
        const code = (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code;
        if (code === "ESRCH") return;
        // Darwin can report EPERM while the keeper's group is retiring.
        // It still means present: wait within the same bound, never claim
        // cleanup success until the kernel reports ESRCH.
        if (code !== "EPERM") throw error;
      }
      const remaining = expires - performance.now();
      if (remaining <= 0) throw Error("proof process-group cleanup timed out after 5000 ms");
      await sleep(Math.min(10, remaining));
    }
  })();
  groupClosures.set(child, cleanup);
  return cleanup;
}
/** @throws partial, invalid or reversed PORT_LO/PORT_HI pair. */
export function portRange(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string {
  const low = env.PORT_LO, high = env.PORT_HI;
  if (low === undefined && high === undefined) return "0";
  if (!low || !high || !/^\d+$/.test(low) || !/^\d+$/.test(high)
      || Number(low) < 1 || Number(high) > 65535 || Number(low) > Number(high)) {
    throw Error("PORT_LO and PORT_HI must both specify an inclusive range, 1 <= low <= high <= 65535");
  }
  return `${Number(low)}-${Number(high)}`;
}
/** App-local evidence survives harness cleanup of its temporary root. */
/** @throws None; returns the resolved explicit or cwd/logs path. */
export function logsDir(options: Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" | "logsDir"> = {}): string {
  return resolve(options.cwd ?? ".", options.logsDir ?? "logs");
}
export function logged(command: string, args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? "."), id = randomUUID();
  const directory = logsDir(options); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });
  const logs = { stdout: join(directory, `${id}.stdout.log`), stderr: join(directory, `${id}.stderr.log`) };
  if (options.secret) writeFileSync(logs.stdout, "stdout withheld: secret command (returned only to caller)\n", { flag: "wx", mode: 0o600 });
  const out = options.secret ? undefined : openSync(logs.stdout, "wx", 0o600), err = openSync(logs.stderr, "wx", 0o600);
  const grouped = options.processGroup === true && process.platform !== "win32";
  // Frontends are already detached group leaders with an in-group keeper.
  // Record their group too, so stop/startup cleanup cannot report success
  // before Vite plugins and other inherited descendants have left custody.
  const privateGroup = process.platform !== "win32" && (grouped || options.detached === true);
  const executable = grouped ? process.execPath : command;
  const executableArgs = grouped ? [resolve(fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-command.js", import.meta.url))), command, ...args] : [...args];
  let child: ChildProcess;
  try { child = spawn(executable, executableArgs, { cwd, detached: grouped || (options.detached ?? false), env: { ...(options.env ?? process.env), SNAPBACK4_PROOF_LAUNCH: id }, stdio: [grouped || options.livenessPipe ? "pipe" : "ignore", options.secret ? "pipe" : out!, err, ...(grouped ? ["ipc" as const] : [])] }); }
  finally { if (out !== undefined) closeSync(out); closeSync(err); }
  if (privateGroup && child.pid) privateGroups.set(child, child.pid);
  // Secret stdout never reaches a file, even briefly. Callers still receive it.
  let stdout = "";
  child.stdout?.setEncoding("utf8");
  child.stdout?.on("data", chunk => { stdout += chunk; });
  register(child);
  child.stdin?.on("error", () => {});
  let error: Error | undefined;
  let actualPid = child.pid;
  let actualCode: number | null = null;
  let actualSignal: NodeJS.Signals | null = null;
  let startedResolve!: () => void, startedReject!: (error: Error) => void, startedSettled = false;
  const started = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => { startedResolve = resolve; startedReject = reject; });
  void started.catch(() => {}); // command() observes close/error instead; host startup awaits this promise.
  const didStart = () => { if (!startedSettled) { startedSettled = true; startedResolve(); } };
  const failedStart = (value: unknown) => { if (!startedSettled) { startedSettled = true; startedReject(value instanceof Error ? value : Error(String(value))); } };
  if (!grouped) didStart();
  child.on("message", message => {
    const event = message as {type?:string;pid?:number;code?:number|null;signal?:NodeJS.Signals|null;error?:string};
    if (event.type === "started" && typeof event.pid === "number") { actualPid = event.pid; didStart(); }
    if (event.type === "result") { actualCode = event.code ?? null; actualSignal = event.signal ?? null; if (event.error) error = Error(event.error); }
  });
  child.on("error", value => { error = value; failedStart(value); });
  const closed = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => child.once("close", () => {
    failedStart(error ?? Error("proof command exited before its process-group handshake"));
    groupGone(child).then(resolve,reject);
  }));
  // Hosts use stopChild() as their cleanup join; commands await closed directly.
  // Observe the promise here as well so an early frontend close cannot create
  // an unhandled rejection before its startup catch reaches stopChild().
  void closed.catch(() => {});
  return { child, logs, id, closed, started, pid: () => actualPid, code: () => grouped ? actualCode : child.exitCode, signal: () => grouped ? actualSignal : child.signalCode, stdout: () => options.secret ? stdout : logText(logs.stdout), error: () => error };
}
export const logText = (path: string): string => readFileSync(path, "utf8");
export async function ready(run: ReturnType<typeof logged>, parse: (line: string) => string | undefined, timeoutMs: number): Promise<string> {
  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let outAt = 0, errAt = 0;
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
    if (run.child.exitCode !== null || run.child.signalCode !== null) {
      throw Error(`proof child exited before ready (${run.child.exitCode ?? run.child.signalCode}); ${run.logs.stderr}\n${logText(run.logs.stderr).slice(-4000)}`);
    }
    for (const [path, offset, stream] of [[run.logs.stdout, outAt, "out"], [run.logs.stderr, errAt, "err"]] as const) {
      const text = logText(path);
      const complete = text.lastIndexOf("\n") + 1;
      for (const line of text.slice(offset, complete).split("\n")) {
        const url = parse(stripVTControlCharacters(line).trim());
        if (url) return url;
      }
      if (stream === "out") outAt = complete; else errAt = complete;
    }
    await sleep(25);
  }
  throw Error(`proof child ready timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; logs: ${run.logs.stdout}, ${run.logs.stderr}`);
}
import { after as teardownAfter } from "./helpers/teardown.mjs";
// H9 controls can load the unchanged 0.4.29 proof/witness modules via KIT2_BASELINE_DIST.
import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { mkdtempSync, rmSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { chromiumExecutable } from '../../../witness/lib.mjs';
const dist = process.env.KIT2_BASELINE_DIST ?? resolve(import.meta.dirname, '../dist');
const kit = await import(pathToFileURL(join(dist, 'proof.js')));
const witnessSource = readFileSync(join(dist, 'witness-web.js'), 'utf8');
async function browser(t, run) {
  const cwd = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'kit2-contract-'));
  teardownAfter(t, () => rmSync(cwd, {recursive:true,force:true}));
  await kit.withBrowser({cwd, executablePath:process.env.CHROME ?? chromiumExecutable()}, async context => run(await context.newPage(), cwd));
}
test('DX9 renderedText defaults to substring matching for a row with more than the needle', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setContent('<section aria-label="Rows"><p>Proof needle — more row text</p><input value="Proof needle"></section>');
  const within = page.getByRole('region', {name:'Rows'});
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page, 'Proof needle — more row text', null).count(), 1, 'null options mean the defaults');
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page, 'Proof needle', {within, exact:true}).count(), 0, 'exact remains opt-in');
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page, 'Proof needle', {within, exact:true}), null, 50), /a substring match exists; pass a RegExp or `exact: false`/);
  const row = kit.renderedText(page, 'Proof needle', {within, exact:false});
  assert.equal(await row.count(), 1, 'explicit substring matches the whole rendered row');
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(row, null, {timeoutMs:500}), 'Proof needle — more row text');
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page, /Proof needle/, {within}), null, 500), 'Proof needle — more row text');
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(row, 'Proof needle — more row text', {timeoutMs:1000}), /locator.*last count=1.*Proof needle.*present but unchanged/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page,'missing'), null, {timeoutMs:1000}), /last count=0.*text=null.*absent/);
}));
test('DX8 renderedText finds a full status line anywhere in a scrollable RN-style root', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setViewportSize({width:700,height:400});
  await page.setContent(`<main style="height:90px;overflow-y:auto"><div style="height:600px"></div><div><span>Pump </span><span>logged.</span></div></main>`);
  const root=page.locator('main');
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Pump logged.',{within:root,exact:false}).count(),1);
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page,'Pump logged.',{within:root,exact:false}),null,500),'Pump logged.');
  assert.ok(await root.evaluate(element=>element.scrollTop)>0,'proof observation scrolls the match into view');
}));

test('DX8 exhaustPages follows opaque cursors and refuses incomplete traversal', async () => {
  const cursors=[];
  const rows=await kit.exhaustPages(async cursor=>{
    cursors.push(cursor);
    if(cursor===null)return {data:[{id:'newest'}],next:'opaque:page-2',complete:true,cut:null};
    return {data:[{id:'older'}],next:null,complete:true,cut:null};
  });
  assert.deepEqual(cursors,[null,'opaque:page-2']);
  assert.deepEqual(rows,[{id:'newest'},{id:'older'}]);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({denied:{code:'PRIVATE',message:'no'}})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*PRIVATE/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({loading:true})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*loading/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({data:[],next:null,cut:null})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*complete must be true/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({data:[],next:'would-continue',complete:false,cut:'meter'})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*incomplete.*meter/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({data:[],next:null,complete:true,cut:null,existence:'partial'})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*partial existence/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({data:[],next:'same',complete:true,cut:null}),{maxPages:2,label:'board'}),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*board.*repeated cursor/);
});
test('DX6 composed RN text and wrapping labels preserve scope, exactness and ambiguity', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setContent(`<section aria-label="Rows"><div><span>Order </span><span>42</span><span> saved</span></div>
    <label><span>Due </span><strong>tomorrow</strong><input value="secret value"></label>
    <p hidden>Order 42 saved</p><p>Order 42 saved later</p></section>`);
  const within = page.getByRole('region', {name:'Rows'});
  for (const text of ['Order 42 saved', /^Order 42 saved$/]) {
    const found = kit.renderedText(page, text, {within,exact:true});
    assert.equal(await found.count(), 1, 'composed text selects the smallest rendered container');
    assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(found, null, 500), 'Order 42 saved');
  }
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page, 'Due tomorrow', {within}).count(), 1, 'wrapping input label composes descendants without its value');
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Due tomorrow',{within:page.locator('label')}).count(),1,'a wrapping label can itself be the scope, as with Playwright getByText');
  assert.equal(await kit.statusText(page, 'Due tomorrow').count(), 1);
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page, 'secret value', {within}).count(), 0);
  await page.getByRole('region',{name:'Rows'}).evaluate(el=>el.insertAdjacentHTML('beforeend', `<style>.invisible{display:none}</style><div><span hidden>Approved</span><button>Save</button></div><div><span class="invisible">Accepted</span><button>Submit</button></div><div><span style="visibility:hidden">Confirmed</span><button>Go</button></div><div><span style="opacity:0">Done</span><button>Finish</button></div><div contenteditable="true">Editable</div><div><span>Visible </span><span hidden>secret </span><strong>composed</strong></div>`));
  for(const text of ['Approved','Accepted','Confirmed','Done','Editable']) for(const needle of [text,new RegExp(text)]) assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,needle,{within}).count(),0,`${text}: hidden or editable text is not evidence`);
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Visible composed',{within}).count(),1,'hidden adornment does not prevent composed visible evidence');
  await page.getByRole('region',{name:'Rows'}).evaluate(el=>el.insertAdjacentHTML('beforeend', `<div><span style="display:block;width:0;height:0;overflow:hidden">Submitted</span><button>Continue</button></div><div><span style="display:block;width:0;height:0;overflow:scroll">Finished</span></div><div><span style="display:block;width:0;height:0;border:100px solid transparent;overflow:hidden">Authorized</span></div><div style="width:10px;overflow:hidden;white-space:nowrap;font:20px monospace">Completed</div><p><span style="display:contents;overflow:hidden">Layoutless </span><span style="display:inline-block;width:0;height:0;overflow:visible">wrapper</span></p>`));
  for(const text of ['Submitted','Finished','Completed','Authorized']) assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,text,{within}).count(),0,`${text}: clipped text cannot manufacture full rendered evidence`);
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Layoutless wrapper',{within}).count(),1,'layoutless and zero-size visible-overflow wrappers retain rendered text');
  const clipped=within.locator('div').filter({has:page.locator('span').filter({hasText:'Submitted'})}).first();
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(clipped,null,500),'Continue','arbitrary locator freshness uses the same clipping rules');
  const visible=kit.renderedText(page,'Visible composed',{within});
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(visible,null,500),'Visible composed');
  await visible.evaluate(el=>el.querySelector('[hidden]').textContent='changed invisible text');
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(visible,'Visible composed',50),/present but unchanged/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(visible.filter({visible:true}),'Visible composed',50),/present but unchanged/);


  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page, 'order 42 saved', {within,exact:true}).count(), 0, 'exact remains case sensitive');
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page, 'Order 42 saved', {within, exact:false}).count(), 2);
  await page.getByRole('region', {name:'Rows'}).evaluate(el => el.insertAdjacentHTML('beforeend', '<p>Order 42 saved</p>'));
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page, 'Order 42 saved', {within}), null, 500), /strict mode violation/);
}));
test('DX6 rendered locators work on caller-owned Chromium outside withBrowser', {timeout:30000}, async t => {
  const {chromium}=createRequire(join(dist,'proof.js'))('playwright-core');

});

# LLP 3000.011.003.000.000: Packaged application proof kit

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 proof tooling, starters, fixtures, release source selection
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Revised:** 2026-10-24 (DX9 wave-26 and DX10 §16 amendments; Muse and fresh Codex READY); 2026-10-23 (DX8: corroborated duplicate strace listener representation, §8); 2026-10-18 (DAY5 amendments; KIT4 clock, proof and test contracts); 2026-10-17 (KIT3 proof and clock contracts); 2026-10-16 (REG missing frontend export diagnostic; §11: wave-14 DAY4); 2026-10-16 (§9: wave-13 DAY3; §10: wave-13 KIT2); 2026-10-16 (§8: explicit platform boundary and client-source guard); 2026-10-15 (§8: wave-12 KIT and observational listener qualification)
**Related:** [Starters](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md), [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Built application proof](./3000.011.005.000-qualify-the-built-application.rfc.md)

STAGE staging and persistent startDev defaults are amended by [LLP 3000.000.000.001](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md); KIT4 owns other ongoing helper changes.

## 15. Wave-26 proof ergonomics (DX9)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-24. Revision 3: Muse and fresh Codex READY; implementation is in the DX9 commits. Qualification and limitations: WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md at the worktree root.
This supersedes string exactness in §§1/5 and the DX6 proof default, retaining
the smallest visible composed-text selector and strict ambiguity refusal.
renderedText strings default to normalized substring matching; exact:true
requests equality and regexes retain their own semantics. Independent matches
are errors naming the ambiguity and advising within or exact:true; never pick
first. waitForFresh rejects two within scopes synchronously with E_PROOF_USAGE,
including a locator originally scoped by renderedText. This avoids silently
searching a descendant scope twice. Native locator scoping remains Playwright's.

fillRN retains keystrokes for ordinary React Native text controls. HTML date,
datetime-local, month, time and week controls use Playwright fill, so React
receives ordinary input/change events. File controls use setInputFiles with
paths or file payloads; invalid input/control combinations fail explicitly.
No DOM value assignment or fabricated receipt establishes a proof.

serveUntilSignal(hosts) requires at least one owned host (otherwise
E_PROOF_USAGE) and holds a referenced timer while waiting for SIGTERM or
SIGINT, then stops all supplied owned hosts in reverse acquisition order using
the kit's independent bounded cleanup. It removes timer/listeners on all exits.
Repeated signals during cleanup use the still-installed same handler and do
not start a second cleanup; listeners are removed after cleanup. A successful
signal shutdown resolves normally (exit 0 in the recipe), and
cleanup failures reject. It never installs process.exit handlers or signals
unowned PIDs; supplied hosts retain DX8's process-group custody and idempotent
stop behavior. Recipe startup/failure cleanup uses finally so pre-helper errors
also release hosts. Test real parent/child lifetimes and signal exit status,
not only a mocked signal emitter. Packed recipe tests run the exact fences.

## 1. Contract

Implementer: PROOF workstream, 2026-10-14. Wave 11 fix list 1 authorizes
one published `snapback4/proof` Node module. It owns orchestration, never client
custody or backend/bundler supervision in the product. Existing `startDev(args)`
and `stopChild(child | {url, stop})` recipes remain usable as module exports.

`startDev`, `startServe(directory)`, `startStatic(directory)` and
`startVite(directory)` return `{url, pid, logs, stop}`. Options carry cwd,
environment, arguments and bounded ready/stop deadlines. Read PORT_LO/PORT_HI
at invocation: absent means an OS-assigned port; exactly one set refuses. With
both set, validate 1 <= LO <= HI <= 65535 and bind the first free port in that
inclusive range; exhaustion refuses. Qualification's occupied first port must
be skipped, never adopted. The existing native SNAPBACK4_READY line already names its direct child PID (LLP 3000.011.004.000); no new readiness log is needed. The listening process binds first and keeps that socket;
never adopt an already-serving port. Vite uses its supported JavaScript server
API in a directly spawned Node child, strict ports, the project's config and
a per-launch identity middleware. The directly spawned frontend calls its
server listen API for each candidate; no probe-and-release step exists. With
no range it calls listen on port 0, which the tested Vite API supports with
strictPort; it reads the actual retained socket address. Static hosting uses a directly spawned Node
child with traversal protection, correct wasm MIME and SPA fallback.

All child stdout/stderr go to separate files beneath `logs/`. Read complete
lines from those files, strip ANSI, bound readiness, and reject premature exit.
Native ready lines must name the directly spawned PID. To assert HTTP identity,
the kit sets a random SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID only in the child environment;
`/auth/config` includes `proof_id` only when this variable is nonempty. It
confers no authority or bypass, and ordinary servers omit it. Both static and Vite frontend child
responses carry an `x-snapback4-proof` marker. Identity validation
precedes returning a handle. No public development door is enabled in serve.

Register every spawned child and browser process for an exit-time SIGKILL
sweep, using retained native ChildProcess handles, not process-name kills.
TERM followed by bounded KILL/reap stops a child. Every cleanup action has
its own bound and later actions still run when an earlier action fails.
`cli(args)` runs the native binary, awaits close, returns separate stdout and
stderr, and has a command deadline. It never runs npm/npx or shell strings.

`withBrowser({executablePath: process.env.CHROME}, callback)` launches Chromium
through playwright-core, closes with a bound, and registers its actual browser
process. Root needs --no-sandbox; other hosts first attempt a sandboxed launch
and retry without it only on recognized sandbox-unavailable diagnostics: "No usable sandbox", "Running as
root without --no-sandbox", "Failed to move to new namespace", "SUID sandbox",
"sandbox ... Operation not permitted", or "Failed to unshare ... Operation not
permitted". Unrelated failures never retry.
`waitForFresh` polls for nonempty changed text; `fillRN` clears and types real
keystrokes; `statusText` uses text selectors; `selectByValue` selects stable
option values. The browser executable is CHROME (when provided), undefined executablePath is omitted from launch options, then Playwright's
installed Chromium is used; an absent executable refuses at launch, with its path.
waitForFresh defaults to 30 seconds. Binary resolution is resolveBinary from
the same installed snapback4 package (override SNAPBACK4_BIN only for explicit
workspace tests); the opt-in identity requires CLI/client 0.4.28 or newer and
an older CLI omits proof_id and therefore fails the exact marker assertion; no inferred version comparison or success claim is made. cli(args) does not
invoke package managers; publication uses its existing guarded command runner.

None interprets an HTTP acknowledgement as rendered settlement.

## 2. Explicit source and aliases

`deploy dir --schema file` compiles exactly that saved Quarry file and stages
those same bytes as canonical snapback/schema.q, preserving operation names
regardless of the saved file's name. Compile diagnostics name the selected input.
This happens without swapping the working schema or generating its types.
It does not concatenate other snapback/*.q files. It allocates ordinary numeric
immutable releases, uses the same .pending-adoption marker, transaction/history,
latest selection, restoration-intent preview and no-op checks as normal deploy;
no alternate activation path or restoration bypass exists. The normal deployment path
retains multi-file behavior and activation semantics. Server/native companion source comes from the current project, while all
schema-dependent generated signatures and native builds run against the selected
schema in a private snapshot. A uniquely created snapshot project has
snapback/schema.q, captured companion source, fresh snapback/generated/
and copied native sources (target caches excluded). Host-installed node_modules
Adapt previews show every rewritten/added/removed file and package changes,
including script ordering and snapback4.starter.day2. Legacy versions/v1.q and
v2.q differences from new or existing directory snapshots are reported, while
both evidence forms remain unchanged. Check identifies inherited assertion
placeholders and todo snapshots by exact shipped digest, and labels custom
npx/readiness-only proof advice heuristic. A Home starter has no todo frontend
proof or command; adapt removes only the exact recognized todo proof and its
managed commands. Custom proofs and READMEs stay application-owned. Upgrade
--quiet retains project actions and errors while suppressing successful package
manager, check and release-history output. PROD2 owns the production adaptation
action text; KIT4 owns the worked day-two proof recipe and helper API table.

Round-one repairs also cover empty flat saved files, acquisition-only residency
requiring --fixture-only because it executes no business cases, replaying both
package-manager failure streams in quiet mode, and wiring the installed DAY5
recipe into Linux and Darwin qualification. Those paths fail on its refusal;
the standalone helper alone is not qualification. LANG4 owns the remaining
compiler-note wording that recommends residency measurement without its new
explicit --fixture-only flag.

Unsaved-source classification uses the I/O error kind, not a filename/message
substring. Snapshot capture itself refuses all-empty source before reservation,
so it cannot manufacture an archive the saved-source loader would reject.

Revision 2: both NOT READY (clock polarity/identity and invalidation precision).
Revision 3: both READY on the same text. The author commissioned autonomous
implementation and extra review rounds in the DX9 workstream brief.

## 16. Wave-27 process checks (DX10)

Implementer: DX10, 2026-10-24, wave-27 §10b #4/#6. `serveCheck` and the packaged
`serve-check --deployment DIR --script NAME` execute `npm run --silent NAME` in
a private Unix group. One JSON object line is required; stdout stays in memory
and never appears in its report/logs because it can contain credentials. Keep
alive for 30 seconds after readiness (configurable), then group SIGTERM. Report
missing JSON, extra output, early exit, remaining live non-zombie children and
unreleased store ownership separately. Inspect group and lock before forced
cleanup, so cleanup cannot erase the failure. The keeper ignores TERM, pins the
group during inspection, and kills only its own group on driver EOF. Detached
children that deliberately leave the group remain outside supported custody.
Windows refuses; Mac execution is deferred to integration. Inspection failure, driver exit or IPC loss before authorized cleanup
rejects, never passes. Stage recipes use the ordinary live-owner control path
or exclusive cold move, and await each operation and owned-host stop.

Production account setup reuses a named local roster under the deployment,
with private fsynced atomic snapshots (file, containing directory and new directory link before effects) and an exclusive per-roster setup lock. The
startServe handle binds its deployment directory; standalone hosts must name
rosterDir. A stable role/count, mutation and service plan is required. Persist
random credentials before signup; on resumption first try login. A known
principal that cannot log in refuses instead of creating a replacement. Persist
principal, arguments and mutation ID before provisioning; replay an unfinished
mutation with that same ID under ordinary server deduplication. Completed
entries only log in, never provision again. Changed wire arguments/plans refuse; argument object key order is canonicalized recursively.
No authority bypass, grant repair or rollback promise is added. Removing or
renaming a roster explicitly requests new identities; preserve it with its
store. A stale exclusive lock requires operator inspection, never automatic
stealing. Staging seedAccounts retains its fresh-batch behavior.

Validation uses real production restarts and interrupted replies, process driver
faults, literal guide stage scripts, and the packed package. Credentials are
never included in committed reports. Gate semantic gaps are handed to SEC10;
DX10's write-advice changes only prioritize the explicit update-only answer.

## 17. Wave-29 serve proofs and proof residue (DX12)

DX12 extends §16's same npm script/process-group/first-JSON-line custody with
`serve-check --then '<cmd>'` and `withServeScript(script, fn, options)`. The
script's readiness JSON stays in memory. A command receives SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON,
SNAPBACK_URL/BACKEND_URL and FRONTEND_URL; a callback receives the object and an
AbortSignal. Neither report nor runner logs persist readiness. The shell command
runs in the same keeper-owned group as the serve, so descendants cannot survive
normal or deadline cleanup without a reported failure. The command's nonzero
exit status is preserved. A successful command cannot erase a serve startup,
lifetime, graceful shutdown or lock-release failure. Startup and proof deadlines
are independent; a hung command names its deadline. Cleanup observes remaining
children and the store lock before forced group reaping, as §16 requires.
Callback failure propagates after cleanup; callback-owned resources must honor
the signal or supply their own bounds. Arbitrary JavaScript promises cannot be
forcibly cancelled. Escaping process groups remain outside custody.

All recipes for testing an existing npm serve point to this runner, including
probe:check, staging and day-two business assertions. Direct startServe remains
a kit primitive for constructing an app's serve script or a disposable kit-owned
multi-host fixture; builders need no custom npm-script driver.

`renderedText` discovers reachable text through legal scroll ranges and scrolls
a unique match into view before observation. Root overflow propagated to the
viewport does not clip Expo web text to the non-scrolling body box. Ambiguity,
hidden/editable text and non-scrollable clips retain their refusals. This
supersedes §12's observation-without-scrolling behavior.

`devAccounts(startDevHost, plan)` (also a DevHost method) uses the same role/count,
provisioning mutation, service seat, custom args and role-keyed credential shape
as production. It requires development password identity, signs up actual users
and invokes ordinary authorized provisioning. It neither seeds tables nor
relaxes production/staging refusal checks. Development batches are fresh;
production roster persistence remains §16's contract and REL2's lock repair.

`beginForm`'s argument object constrains its named keys: handler-owned extra
arguments are accepted, nested named values still compare canonical JSON
exactly, and null/undefined leaves arguments unconstrained. This supersedes
§10's whole-object equality and fixes partial claims dropping the receipt.
Trusted click/synchronous submit, handler claims, durable-op selection and
actual write receipts remain required. Named value mismatches still settle an
explicit E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED receipt without preventing the application's write.

Verification uses packed consumers for changed recipes and starters, process
fault/deadline/status/lock tests, development real-user reads, and browser tests
of Expo root scrolling, hidden/ambiguous text and subset/value mismatch claims.
Wave-29 replay inputs are copied read-only from the evidence into temporary
workspaces, measurements exclude credentials, and copies are deleted afterwards.
Darwin/native execution is skipped in this Linux workstream.
fixtures. Schema-typed text, bytes, JSON, asset values and unknown compound
values are replaced by <redacted>; argument strings are redacted even when an
enum. Retained scalar numerics/booleans, generated principals and row ids,
source enum state names, operation names and candidate predicates describe
the behavior. History records contain sanitized arguments and state only.
A sentinel regression checks opaque redaction. Witness caches are generated
presentation evidence and never decide authority or a verdict.

Ordinary default check keeps the existing 512-work/1500ms campaign limit;
it starts no additional witness campaign. Cached concrete question evidence is
shown for gates when available. Explicit --review, --verbose, JSON and
--ack-security request a separate, deterministic, fixed 8192-work generated
question campaign (plus at most two direct concretization writes per numeric question), cached by selected source/notes and executable digest. This
opt-in budget supersedes §17.2 only for this presentation-evidence search.
Exhaustion keeps witnesses unavailable, retains every gate/refutation and
never changes an ID. A first uncached review can take seconds; default check
pays no expanded campaign. Source/fixture-authored refutations still have their
own fast campaign and complete test command. The new evidence is not a
qualification campaign and confers no proof status.

The adjoining developer-loop changes preserve the contracts of LLP
3000.000.000.001 and LLP 3000.015: refusal attribution and read-filter evidence
are developer-owned diagnostics, never new disclosure in public refusal
responses. Hooks expose `usePage.loading` and `usePage.why` as projections of
`page.read`, retaining its completeness/freshness evidence. A first-run check
hint makes the pinned React starter discoverable for projects that skipped
init, without modifying their files. Staging reports name persisted clock
offset; upload overload guidance names serialization/retry; proof docs say
text must be on screen, and verification recipes use `serve-check`. Expo's
starter offers a web file picker and native image picker.

Qualification: targeted CLI/client regressions, witnesses for wave-28 011 F1,
011 F2 and 019 F1, copies of all fifty schemas with baseline and SEC10 codes,
packed consumer snippets/starters, affected selector and reference check.
Darwin is unavailable in this Linux lane and is explicitly skipped. Finite
campaign replay is not a security proof or a promise of builder preference.
The CLI tests cover all JSON decision IDs, atomic mixed-invalid batches,
comment/independent-edit stability, historical family additions, real grant
attribution without public disclosure and authored-refutation precedence.
React lifecycle tests exercise loading, denied and stale/partial page evidence;
upload retry tests retain retry bounds and assert guidance. Packed tests run
staging/probe scripts and typecheck/export Expo's picker. Clock tests check the
printed retained offset. The 450-project new-gate census is in SEC10's lane:
DX11 introduces no gate. --review retains the compact nondecision notes and
individual questions (including the complete audience-family site list);
JSON retains original diagnostics, never an invented questionnaire ID.
Review records: [Muse round 1](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-muse-r1-20261025.md)
requested precision fixes; [fresh Codex round 1](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-codex-r1-20261025.md)
was READY. [Muse round 2](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-muse-r2-20261025.md) and
[fresh Codex round 2](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-codex-r2-20261025.md) were READY
with the opt-in campaign-budget clarification carried into
[Muse round 3](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-muse-r3-20261025.md) and
[fresh Codex round 3](./reviews/3000.036.004-dx11-codex-r3-20261025.md), both READY.
All transcripts are retained verbatim with provenance. Implementation
qualification belongs to the DX11 workstream report, not these design verdicts.

### 19.1 Deploy, rollback and release inspection (DX12)

Apply §19's short default to deploy, rollback and releases. Completed activation
prints verdict then REL's `live:` identity. A refused command prints the refusal
verdict and actionable gates/refutations; derived acknowledged candidates remain
review evidence. Default deploy uses the same `render_mode` decision filter as
check. Advisory decisions move behind --review with a one-line count and next
command. --verbose also retains them and expands previews; successful activation
does not reprint its validation preview. Explicit --check keeps visibility and
hazards with next steps; verbose expands grouped notes and storage detail.
`releases DIR` prints compact identity/evidence counts; --json preserves its
structured contract and --review/--verbose retains expanded inspection. JSON
security reports keep all decisions and evidence. Gate registry, binding,
acknowledgment and release semantics are unchanged; SEC and REL own those.

Verification compares wave-29 copied schemas' deploy transcripts before/after,
asserts verdict/live ordering, advisory opt-in and structured evidence, and
retains a blocking-gate regression with its repair and acknowledgment commands.

//! A family command expands to current individual, source-bound records.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#18-wave-26-security-decisions-dx9 — review questions lead, and gates never enter the audience family.
use super::*;
use snapback4_lang::Diagnostic;

pub(super) fn audience(code: &str) -> bool {
    !gated(code) && matches!(code, "N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE" | "N_ROLE_WIDE_READ" | "N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE" | "N_BROAD_READ")
}
pub(super) fn family(notes: &[Diagnostic], identities: &[(String,String,String)]) -> Option<String> {
    let mut members = notes.iter().zip(identities).filter(|(note,_)| audience(&note.code))
        .map(|(_, (id,source,_))| (id,source)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if members.is_empty() { return None; }
    members.sort();
    Some(format!("audience:{}", hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&(ANALYZER,members)).unwrap())))
}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn count(dispositions: &[Disposition]) -> usize {
    let audience = dispositions.iter().any(|d| !d.acknowledged && d.family.is_some());
    usize::from(audience) + dispositions.iter().filter(|d| !d.acknowledged && d.family.is_none()
        && crate::note_rank::tier(&d.code)==crate::note_rank::Tier::Review).count()
}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn print(dispositions: &[Disposition], notes: &[Diagnostic]) {print!("{}",render(dispositions,notes));}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn render(dispositions: &[Disposition], notes: &[Diagnostic]) -> String {
    use std::fmt::Write;
    let mut output=String::new();
    let mut ordered=notes.iter().zip(dispositions).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#165-review-clarification-exact-detector-and-command-boundaries — grouping must preserve gate/authority priority.
    ordered.sort_by_key(|(n,_)|(crate::note_rank::summary_order(&n.code),n.site.file.clone(),n.site.line,n.site.col));
    let members=ordered.iter().filter(|(_,d)|d.family.is_some()&&!d.acknowledged).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let mut family_output=String::new();
    if let Some((_,first))=members.first().copied() {
        let codes=members.iter().map(|(n,_)|n.code.as_str()).collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        writeln!(&mut family_output,"decision audience ({codes}): Are these {} row/column audiences, roles and lifecycle states intended?",members.len()).unwrap();
        for (n,_) in &members {
            // The expanded branch cross-product stays in JSON. One question
            // names the source subject without repeating every equivalent path.
            let subject = n.message.split(" matches or weakens").next().unwrap_or(&n.message)
                .split(": ").next().unwrap_or(&n.message);
            writeln!(&mut family_output,"  ? {}: Is {} intended?",n.site,subject.trim_end_matches('.')).unwrap();
        }
        writeln!(&mut family_output,"  Intended: snapback4 check --ack-security {} --reason '<spec rationale>'",first.family.as_deref().unwrap()).unwrap();
        writeln!(&mut family_output,"  Narrower: restrict the listed rules; declare independent intent; run snapback4 test. Full audiences: check --json.").unwrap();
    }
    let mut family_printed=false;
    for (n,d) in ordered {
        if d.acknowledged {if let Some(w)=&d.witness {writeln!(&mut output,"  Witness recorded: {}",w["summary"].as_str().unwrap_or("see check --json")).unwrap();} writeln!(&mut output,"security reviewed {} {}: {}",d.id,d.code,d.reason.as_deref().unwrap_or("")).unwrap(); continue; }
        if d.family.is_some() {
            if !family_printed {output.push_str(&family_output);family_printed=true;}
            continue;
        }
        if !d.gated && crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review { continue; }
        let subject=n.message.split(": ").next().unwrap_or(&n.message);
        let concise=match n.code.as_str() {
            "N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE"=>format!("{subject}: should operation results/refusals expose protected or projected-out columns to every admitted caller"),
            _=>n.message.trim_end_matches(['.', '?']).to_owned(),
        };
        let concise=concise.replace("insert/upsert", "insert, or edit an existing row via upsert");
        let question=concise.trim_end_matches(['.', '?']);
        writeln!(&mut output,"decision {} at {}: {question}; is this intended?",d.code,d.site).unwrap();
        if let Some(witness)=&d.witness {writeln!(&mut output,"  Witness: {}",witness["summary"].as_str().unwrap_or("concrete campaign behavior; check --json for replay")).unwrap();}
        else if matches!(n.code.as_str(),"N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED"|"N_ARGUMENT_GRANT"|"N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT"|"N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY") {writeln!(&mut output,"  Witness: none obtained in this bounded campaign; `check --review` searches for concrete behavior; this remains a specification question").unwrap();}
        let repair=match n.code.as_str() {
            "N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE"=>"restrict result/refusal audiences with column rules and independent intent; run snapback4 test",
            "N_ARGUMENT_GRANT"=>"validate the recipient's current role and relationship; caller authority alone is insufficient",
            _=>n.rewrite.as_str(),
        };
        writeln!(&mut output,"  Repair: {repair}").unwrap();
        writeln!(&mut output,"  Intended: snapback4 check --ack-security {} --reason '<spec rationale>'{}",d.id,if d.gated {" (required before check can pass)"}else{""}).unwrap();
    }
    output
}

/// One mode selector for the CLI and captured-analyzer format replays.
pub(in crate::verbs) fn render_mode(dispositions:&[Disposition],notes:&[Diagnostic],review:bool)->String {
    let selected=dispositions.iter().zip(notes).filter(|(d,_)|review || d.recorded || d.gated&&!d.acknowledged).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    render(&selected.iter().map(|(d,_)|(*d).clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>(),&selected.iter().map(|(_,n)|(*n).clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>())
}
#[cfg(test)]mod tests {
 use super::*;
 #[test]fn authority_and_gates_precede_one_complete_audience_family(){
  let codes=["N_BROAD_READ","N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION","N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"];
  let notes=codes.iter().map(|code|Diagnostic::new(&Default::default(),*code,"subject: review authority","repair enforcement")).collect::<Vec<_>>();
  let dispositions=codes.iter().map(|code|Disposition{family:audience(code).then(||"audience:family".into()),id:(*code).into(),code:(*code).into(),site:"schema.q:1:1".into(),gated:gated(code),acknowledged:false,recorded:false,reason:None,witness:None,scope:"test".into()}).collect::<Vec<_>>();
  let text=render(&dispositions,&notes);
  assert!(text.find("decision N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE").unwrap()<text.find("decision N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION").unwrap(),"{text}");
  assert!(text.find("decision N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION").unwrap()<text.find("decision audience").unwrap(),"{text}");
  assert_eq!(text.matches("decision audience").count(),1,"{text}");
  assert!(text.contains("Are these 2 row/column audiences")&&text.contains("audience:family"),"{text}");
 }
 #[test]fn every_gate_is_one_decision_and_never_a_family_member(){
  for code in snapback4_lang::security::GATED_NOTE_CODES {
   assert!(!audience(code));
   let n=Diagnostic::new(&Default::default(),*code,"explicit review required","repair enforcement");
   let d=Disposition{family:None,id:"id".into(),code:(*code).into(),site:"schema.q:1:1".into(),gated:true,acknowledged:false,recorded:false,reason:None,witness:None,scope:"test".into()};
   let text=render(&[d],&[n]);assert_eq!(text.matches(code).count(),1,"{text}");assert!(text.contains("is this intended?")&&text.contains("Repair:")&&text.contains("Intended:"));
  }
  for code in ["N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY","N_CALLER_TIME","N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE","N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED","E_SECURITY_REVIEW"] {assert!(!audience(code));}
 }
}

#[cfg(test)]mod sec10_replay {
 use super::*;
 fn diagnostic(n:&serde_json::Value)->Diagnostic {Diagnostic::new(&snapback4_lang::Site::new(n["site"]["file"].as_str().unwrap(),n["site"]["line"].as_u64().unwrap() as u32,n["site"]["col"].as_u64().unwrap() as u32),n["code"].as_str().unwrap(),n["message"].as_str().unwrap(),n["rewrite"].as_str().unwrap())}
 fn disposition(n:&Diagnostic,gated:bool)->Disposition {Disposition{family:None,id:n.code.clone(),code:n.code.clone(),site:n.site.to_string(),gated,acknowledged:false,recorded:false,reason:None,scope:"captured SEC10 source-bound decision".into(),witness:None}}
 #[test]fn captured_sec10_codes_keep_every_gate_and_hide_advisories_by_default(){
  let codes:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_str(include_str!("../../../tests/fixtures/dx11/sec10-codes.json")).unwrap();
  for n in codes.as_object().unwrap().values(){let n=diagnostic(n);let d=disposition(&n,false);assert!(render_mode(&[d],&[n.clone()],false).is_empty());let d=disposition(&n,true);let output=render_mode(&[d],&[n.clone()],false);assert!(output.contains(&n.code)&&output.contains("Intended:"),"{output}");}
 }
 /// Opt-in measurement on all 50 actual SEC10 structured reports. No analyzer
 /// or source is modified: only the production presentation function runs.
 #[test]fn captured_sec10_measurement(){
  let Ok(path)=std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_DX11_SEC10_REPORTS")else{return;};
  let rows:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_slice(&std::fs::read(path).unwrap()).unwrap();let mut measured=Vec::new();
  for row in rows.as_array().unwrap(){
   let notes=row["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().map(diagnostic).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   let ds=row["dispositions"].as_array().unwrap().iter().zip(&notes).map(|(d,n)|disposition(n,d["gated"]==true)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   let gate_count=ds.iter().filter(|d|d.gated).count();
   let project=crate::project::Project{root:std::path::PathBuf::from(format!("/tmp/dx11-replay/sec10/{}",row["lane"].as_str().unwrap()))};
   let sources=project.sources().unwrap();let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&sources);assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty());
   let mut report=snapback4_lang::security::analyze_rules(&bound);report.notes=notes.clone();
   let advice=ds.iter().filter(|d|!d.gated&&crate::note_rank::tier(&d.code)==crate::note_rank::Tier::Review).count();
   let verdict=crate::presentation::check_verdict(gate_count>0,&crate::verbs::table_counts(&bound.schema),bound.schema.synced().count(),bound.programs.len(),if gate_count==0{"compiled"}else{"security review required"},gate_count,advice,0,row["noteCount"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0) as usize,row["ms"].as_f64().unwrap(),crate::verbs::init::client_setup_hint(&project.root),"");
   let text=format!("{verdict}\n{}\n{}",crate::verbs::security::enforced_text(&report,&bound),render_mode(&ds,&notes,false));
   let lines=text.lines().count();if gate_count==0{assert!(lines<=5);}
   measured.push(serde_json::json!({"lane":row["lane"],"exit":if gate_count==0{0}else{1},"lines":lines,"chars":text.chars().count(),"gates":gate_count}));
  }
  if let Ok(path)=std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_DX11_RENDER_OUTPUT"){std::fs::write(path,serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&measured).unwrap()).unwrap();}
 }
}
/** @throws observation deadline with scoped locator, last text and visible headings; selector ambiguity is propagated. */
export async function waitForFresh(locator: Locator, previous: string | null, options: number | { within?: Locator; timeoutMs?: number } = 30_000): Promise<string> {
  const timeoutMs = deadline(typeof options === "number" ? options : options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
  let substring = substringLocators.get(locator);
  locator = registeredLocator(locator);
  if (typeof options !== "number" && options.within) {
    if (scopedLocators.has(locator)) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: within was supplied twice; scope renderedText or waitForFresh once");
    locator = registeredLocator(options.within.locator(locator));
    if (substring) substring = { locator: options.within.locator(substring.locator), scoped: true };
  }
  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let count = 0, text: string | null = null, detail = "";
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    try {
      count = await bounded(locator.count(), Math.max(1, end - performance.now()), "locator count");
      if (count > 1) throw Error(`E_PROOF_AMBIGUOUS: strict mode violation: ${count} rendered matches; use a narrower within or exact: true: ${String(locator)}`);
      text = count ? await bounded(visibleText(locator, Math.max(1, Math.min(1000, end - performance.now()))), Math.max(1, end - performance.now()), "locator text") : null;
      if (text && text !== previous) return text;
    } catch (error) {
      detail = error instanceof Error && (error.name === "TimeoutError" || /timed out after/.test(error.message))
        ? "locator observation exceeded the remaining proof deadline" : String(error);
      // A strictness/selector error needs its original repair, not a timeout.
      if (error instanceof Error && error.name !== "TimeoutError" && !/timed out after/.test(error.message)) throw error;
    }
    await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(25, Math.max(1, end - performance.now()))));
  }
  const hint = count === 0 && substring?.scoped && await bounded(substring.locator.count(), 1000, "substring count").catch(() => 0)
    ? "; a substring match exists; pass a RegExp or `exact: false`" : "";
  const headings = await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(() => locator.page().locator('h1:visible, h2:visible, [role="heading"]:visible').allTextContents()), 1000, "visible headings").catch(() => []);
  const heading = headings.map(text => text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim()).filter(Boolean).slice(0, 3).join(" | ").slice(0, 300);
  throw Error(`no fresh signal after ${timeoutMs} ms: ${String(locator)}; last count=${count}, text=${JSON.stringify(text)}; ${count ? "present but unchanged" : "absent"}; previous=${JSON.stringify(previous)}; visible heading=${JSON.stringify(heading || "(none)")}${hint}${detail ? `; ${detail}` : ""}`);
}
/** Poll authoritative server truth after a receipt; a pending prediction is not a commit. */
/** @throws deadline naming label and last value; read or predicate errors propagate. */
export async function waitForServer<T>(read: () => Promise<T>, accepts: (value: T) => boolean, options: { timeoutMs?: number; label?: string } = {}): Promise<T> {
  const timeoutMs = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000), end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let last: T | undefined;
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
    try {
      const value = await Promise.race([Promise.resolve().then(read), new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
        timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`${options.label ?? "server truth"} timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; last=${JSON.stringify(last)}`)), Math.max(1, end - performance.now()));
      })]);
      last = value;
      if (accepts(value)) return value;
    } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
    await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(25, Math.max(1, end - performance.now()))));
  }
  throw Error(`${options.label ?? "server truth"} timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; last=${JSON.stringify(last)}`);
}
/** Wait until the page's own client has sampled an advanced server clock. Does not advance or replace that clock. */
/** @throws E_PROOF_USAGE for invalid advanced time; missing/disabled witness, signed-out client, page error or page-clock deadline. */
export async function waitForClock(page: Page, advancedNow: number, options: { timeoutMs?: number } = {}): Promise<number> {
  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(advancedNow) || advancedNow < 0) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: waitForClock requires the clock advance reply's now");
  return waitForServer(() => page.evaluate(() => {
    const witness = (window as unknown as { __snapback4Witness?: { clock(): number } }).__snapback4Witness;
    if (!witness?.clock) throw Error("E_WITNESS_DISABLED: registerWebWitness and use witness=web before waitForClock");
    return witness.clock();
  }), now => now >= advancedNow, { ...options, label: "page client clock" });
}
/** @throws locator ambiguity, disabled/closed control, or Playwright deadline. */
export async function fillRN(locator: Locator, value: string | Parameters<Locator["setInputFiles"]>[0]): Promise<void> {
  const type = await locator.evaluate(element => element instanceof HTMLInputElement ? element.type : "text");
  if (type === "file") { await locator.setInputFiles(value); return; }
  if (typeof value !== "string") throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: file paths/payloads require a file input");
  if (["date", "datetime-local", "time", "month", "week"].includes(type)) { await locator.fill(value); return; }
  await locator.focus(); await locator.press("ControlOrMeta+A"); await locator.press("Backspace");
  await locator.pressSequentially(value);
}
/** @throws None at construction; ambiguous text is refused when observed. */
export function statusText(page: Page, text: string | RegExp = /^(?:Ready|pending|sent|failed)$/): Locator { return renderedText(page, text); }
/** @throws locator ambiguity, unavailable option or Playwright deadline. */

pub(super) fn print_release(summary:Option<&String>){if let Some(s)=summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()){eprintln!("{}",s["notice"].as_str().unwrap_or(""));}}
pub(super) fn release_verdict(summary:Option<&String>)->String {summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()).and_then(|s|s["verdict"].as_str().map(str::to_owned)).unwrap_or_else(||"security unchanged".into())}

pub(super) fn enforce_release(summary:Option<&String>,options:&Options)->Result<(),String>{
    let Some(value)=summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok())else{return Ok(())};
    if !options.flags.contains_key("staging")||options.flags.contains_key("strict"){
        if value["fixes"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0)>0{return Err("E_SECURITY_REVIEW: production deploy refuses reachable FIX; repair the named rule and rerun snapback4 test; for a derived candidate only, run check --ack-security ID --reason '<specification rationale>' using the reported rule ID".into())}
        if value["pending"].as_bool()==Some(true){return Err("E_SECURITY_REVIEW: production deploy requires individual security review; run each reported check --ack-security ID --reason '<specification decision>', or enforce the named intent in source".into())}
    }Ok(())
}
//! Operator preflight runs the real activation on a private disk-backed snapshot.
// REL3 owns dormant-visibility counts here; SURF clause narration lives in release_narration.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#92-exact-preview-and-operator-vocabulary — no live pointer, generation or audit changes.
use super::*;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
struct Snapshot(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Snapshot {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
    }
}

pub(super) fn plan(
    store_path: &Path,
    root: PathBuf,
    release: u64,
    options: &Options,
    held_owner: Option<&crate::store_owner::Owner>,
) -> Result<serde_json::Value, String> {
    if let Some(path)=options.flags.get("bridge") {
        let dir=store_path.parent().ok_or("no deployment directory")?;
        let mut store=snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open_read_only(store_path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        let current=store.release_status(0,None,None).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?["release"].as_u64().ok_or("a bridge needs a current release")?;
        super::release_bridge::write(Path::new(path),dir,current,release,&[])?;
        eprintln!("bridge starter written to {path}; follow ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md before selecting exact source");
    }
    let bound = super::releases::bound_at(root.clone())?;
    let project = Project { root };
    let native = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project,&bound.schema)?;
    let id = snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default())
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let snapshot = Snapshot(std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("snapback4-release-preview-{id}")));
    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&snapshot.0).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let path = snapshot.0.join("store.sqlite");
    // Probe the existing filesystem lock, independently of any HTTP address
    // hint. Keep a stopped store excluded through preview; never create a lock
    // sidecar or mutate its address record just to inspect ownership.
    let (live,_stopped_guard)=if held_owner.is_some() {(false,None)} else if store_path.exists() {
        let database=store_path.canonicalize().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        match snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(&database,&snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_path(&database),false,0) {
            Ok(owner)=>match owner.try_lock() {
                Ok(())=>(false,Some(owner)),
                Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock)=>(true,None),
                Err(std::fs::TryLockError::Error(e))=>return Err(format!("preview owner probe failed: {e}")),
            },
            Err(e) if e.kind()==std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound=>return Err("preview cannot establish exclusive store ownership: missing owner sidecar; restart serve once, stop it, then retry".into()),
            Err(e)=>return Err(format!("preview owner probe failed: {e}")),
        }
    }else{(false,None)};
    // SQLite VACUUM INTO takes a coherent read snapshot even with a live WAL writer.
    // Busy/disk errors refuse this preview; never fall back to copying database bytes.
    if store_path.exists() {
    let source = snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open_read_only(store_path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    source
        .snapshot_to(&path)
        .map_err(|e| {
            format!("snapshot preview failed (retry after store/disk becomes available): {e}")
        })?;
    drop(source);
    }
    let mut store = snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    store.preview_asset_root(&store_path.parent().ok_or("preview needs a deployment directory")?.join("assets")).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let held = store
        .release_status(0, None, None)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — preview uses the owning host's authoritative time, without minting or advancing anything.
    let activation_now=if live {
        let status=crate::release_control::request(store_path.parent().ok_or("preview store has no deployment directory")?,crate::release_control::Command::Status{after:0,items_after:None,event:None})?;
        if status["revision"]!=held["revision"] {return Err("preview raced release activation; retry against the current store".into());}
        status["clock"]["now"].as_i64().ok_or("serving owner does not expose its activation clock; restart it with this CLI before preview")?
    }else{snapback4_core::now_ms()};
    let mut activation =
        snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(release, held["release"].as_u64());
    activation.visibility=super::releases::visibility_policy(&project.root)?.or_else(||options.flags.contains_key("visible-rollback").then(Default::default));
    activation.expected_revision = held["revision"].as_u64();
    activation.native_digest = native.installed.map(|n| n.digest);
    activation.operation = "preview".into();
    activation.restore_dormant = !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") || options.flags.contains_key("restore-dormant");
    let mut steps = Vec::new();
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#21-wave-27-release-semantics-rel — workflow source and selected identity are separate facts.
fn live_identity(dir:&Path,release:u64)->Result<serde_json::Value,String>{
    let view=super::rollback_views::metadata(&dir.join("releases").join(release.to_string()))?;
    Ok(match view {
        Some(view)=>serde_json::json!({"release":release,"source":view.target,"parent":view.parent,"view":release,"target":view.target,"exact":false}),
        None=>serde_json::json!({"release":release,"source":release,"view":null,"target":release,"exact":true}),
    })
}
fn print_live(live:&serde_json::Value){
    if live["exact"]==true {println!("live: release {} = exact source {}",live["release"],live["source"]);}
    else {println!("live: release {} = rollback view of release {} (registered by release {})",live["release"],live["target"],live["parent"]);}
}

    fn legacy_view_advice_uses_creator_version_instead_of_manifest_absence() {
        let dir=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("rel-view-version-{}-{}",std::process::id(),snapback4_core::now_ms()));
        std::fs::create_dir(&dir).unwrap();
        for (version,expected) in [("0.4.38",true),("0.4.39",false),("0.4.40",false),("0.5.0",false),("unknown",false)] {

            assert_eq!(super::parent_predates_views(&dir),expected,"{version}");
        }
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(dir).unwrap();

pub(super) fn exposure(report:&Report){
    let broad=report.exposures.iter().filter(|e|e.effects.get("read").is_some_and(|r|r=="allow"||r.starts_with("public"))).count();
    eprintln!("security exposure: {} tables, {} conditional audiences, {broad} broadly readable tables; run snapback4 audit for exact role/column predicates",report.exposures.len(),report.roles.len());
}
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#18-wave-26-security-decisions-dx9 — report enforcement facts without claiming complete security.
pub(super) fn enforced(report:&Report,bound:&Bound){println!("{}",enforced_text(report,bound));}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn enforced_text(report:&Report,bound:&Bound)->String {
    let roles=report.notes.iter().filter(|n|n.code=="N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION").count();
    let columns=bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(_,t)|t.column_reads.len()).sum::<usize>();
    let uploads=bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(_,t)|t.columns.values()).filter(|t|t.contains_asset()).count();
    format!("check: enforced by the language — {uploads} typed upload columns with owner-bound placement; {columns} column audiences; {roles} possible self-assignable role paths detected (bounded analysis)")
}
pub(super) fn summary(report:&Report,verbose:bool){
    let proved=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Proved).count();
    let failures=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability.is_reachable())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let refuted=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted).count();
    let rule_enforced=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved&&v.direct_rule==security::Status::Proved).count();
    eprintln!("security: {proved} proved, {refuted} refuted; {rule_enforced} enforced by rule (additional paths not proved); {} other unproved ({} failing)",report.invariants.len()-proved-refuted-rule_enforced,failures.len());
    for v in &failures {
        let c=v.counterexample.as_ref().unwrap();
    }
    hazards.extend(client_compatibility.iter().filter(|line|!snapback4_lang::client_contracts::is_note(line)).cloned());
    notes.extend(client_compatibility.iter().filter(|line|snapback4_lang::client_contracts::is_note(line)).cloned());
    let checklist = super::release_bridge::checklist(held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0),release,&hazards);
    let payload=serde_json::json!({"sourceRelease":options.flags.get("source-release").and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()),"preview":true,"security":options.flags.get("security-summary").and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()),"visibility":visibility,"rollbackVisibility":rollback_visibility,"rollbackViews":rollback_views,"rollbackViewEffects":view_effects,"rollbackView":options.flags.get("rollback-view").and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()),"clientCompatibility":client_compatibility,"checklist":checklist,"hazards":hazards,"notes":notes,"current":held,"plan":planned,"steps":steps,"estimate":estimate,"phases":engine.store().adoption_report().summary(),"backfills":engine.store().adoption_report().backfills});
    if !options.json&&options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") && !options.flags.contains_key("validation-only") {
        let hidden=view_effects.iter().filter_map(|v|v["visibility"]["hiddenRows"].as_u64()).sum::<u64>();
        println!("deploy verdict: READY; {} rollback views, {hidden} hidden old-table rows across those views; {}",rollback_views.len(),super::security::release_verdict(options.flags.get("security-summary")));
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("validation-only") {
        if !options.json && options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") {for line in visibility.lines(held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0),release) {eprintln!("{line}");}}
            super::release_summary::print(prior_schema.as_ref(),&candidate_schema,&candidate_programs,&snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compact(&hazards),&notes,&steps,&items);
        }
        return Ok(payload);
    }

    if options.json {
        println!("{}",serde_json::to_string_pretty(&payload).unwrap());
    } else {
        if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") {
            for line in visibility.lines(held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0),release) {println!("{line}");}
        }
        if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") {println!("rollback verdict: READY; preview only; live release unchanged");}
        println!("preview: release {release} would be current as generation {}; live release unchanged", engine.generation());
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            super::release_summary::print(prior_schema.as_ref(), &candidate_schema, &candidate_programs, &snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compact(&hazards), &notes, &steps, &items);
        } else {
            for hazard in snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compact(&hazards) {println!("  WARNING {hazard}; next: prove the affected cases before activation; --verbose for the full preview");}
        }
        if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") { println!("  checklist: prove the named refusal cases and affected lists/counts as real accounts before release {release}; `--bridge <new-dir>` saves loosened target source plus this app's ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md"); }
        println!("  storage: {}",snapback4_core::store_port::report::AdoptionReport::dormant(&items));
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            for hazard in hazards { println!("  detail: WARNING {hazard}"); }
            for note in notes { println!("  detail: note: {note}"); }
            for step in steps {println!("  {}: {}",step.path,step.message);}
            for item in &items { println!("  {}: {} {} dormant (restored on compatible forward); sample ids [{}]", item.key, item.rows, item.unit(), item.sample_ids.join(", ")); }
            println!("{}", engine.store().adoption_report().summary());
            if let Some(estimate) = estimate {
                println!("{estimate}");
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(payload)
}

pub(super) fn preview(store_path:&Path,root:PathBuf,release:u64,options:&Options,held_owner:Option<&crate::store_owner::Owner>)->Result<(),String>{plan(store_path,root,release,options,held_owner).map(|_|())}
//! Immutable source releases with a database-authoritative activation pointer.
use super::*;
use crate::store_owner::Owner;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
#[path = "staging.rs"]
mod staging;
struct Stage(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Stage {fn drop(&mut self){let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}

fn parent_predates_views(root:&Path)->bool {
    if root.join("rollback-views.json").exists(){return false;}
    std::fs::read(root.join("provenance.json")).ok().and_then(|bytes|serde_json::from_slice::<serde_json::Value>(&bytes).ok())
        .and_then(|v|v["cliVersion"].as_str().map(str::to_owned))
        .and_then(|v|v.split('.').map(str::parse::<u64>).collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>().ok())
        .is_some_and(|v|v.len()==3&&(v[0],v[1],v[2])<(0,4,39))
}

fn directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    options
        .positionals
        .first()
        .map(PathBuf::from)
        .ok_or_else(|| "a deployment directory is required".into())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5.1 — deployment aliases share ownership, control and worker paths.
pub(super) fn canonical_directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    let requested = directory(options)?;
    if !requested.exists() {
        snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&requested).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    }
    let dir = requested.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&dir).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    Ok(dir)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#8 — validate before any writable ownership or command lock.
pub(crate) fn existing_deployment(requested: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    let missing = || format!("no current release in {}; run `snapback4 deploy {}` first", requested.display(), requested.display());
    if !requested.join("store.sqlite").is_file() { return Err(missing()); }
    let dir = requested.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    inspect_current(&dir)?;
    Ok(dir)
}

// SQLite READONLY can create WAL sidecars. A stopped store's existing owner
// lock makes immutable inspection safe; a live host supplies its own status.
fn inspect_current(dir: &Path) -> Result<u64, String> {
    let database = dir.join("store.sqlite").canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let missing = || format!("no current release in {}; run `snapback4 deploy {}` first", dir.display(), dir.display());
    let owner = match snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(&database,
        &snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_path(&database), false, 0) {
        Ok(owner) => owner,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
            // Relocated/legacy deployments may not yet have a sidecar beside
            // the resolved DB. Establish a complete current source first; only
            // a known deployment may let SQLite create its ordinary WAL files.
            return pointer_current(dir, &database);
        },
        Err(error) => return Err(format!("{}; existing ownership sidecar unavailable: {error}", missing())),
    };
    match owner.try_lock() {
        Ok(()) => {},
        Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) => {
            match crate::release_control::request(dir, crate::release_control::Command::Status {after:0, items_after:None, event:None}) {
                Ok(status) => return status["release"].as_u64().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing),
                Err(error) => {
                    // A killed host's watchdogs retain ownership during their
                    // bounded cleanup. Preserve serve's existing restart wait.
                    if !crate::store_owner::stopped_host(&database) {
                        // A second source directory may alias the same DB. Its
                        // source cannot control this host, but its token still
                        // belongs to the resolved database. Establish current
                        // source before permitting ordinary read-only WAL work.
                        return pointer_current(dir, &database).map_err(|_| format!("{}; {error}",missing()));
                    }
                    let deadline=std::time::Instant::now()+std::time::Duration::from_secs(5);
                    loop {
                        match owner.try_lock() {
                            Ok(()) => break,
                            Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) if std::time::Instant::now()<deadline => std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(25)),
                            _ => return Err(format!("{}; {error}",missing())),
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        },
        Err(error) => return Err(error.to_string()),
    }
    if !snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_is_named(&database, &owner) {
        return Err("store ownership changed during release inspection; retry".into());
    }
    #[cfg(unix)]
    let bytes = { use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt; database.as_os_str().as_bytes() };
    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    let bytes = database.to_str().ok_or("invalid store path")?.as_bytes();
    let mut uri = String::from("file:");
    for byte in bytes {
        if byte.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b"/._-".contains(byte) { uri.push(*byte as char); }
        else { uri.push_str(&format!("%{byte:02X}")); }
    }
    let mut wal = database.as_os_str().to_os_string(); wal.push("-wal"); let wal = PathBuf::from(wal);
    let mut shm = database.as_os_str().to_os_string(); shm.push("-shm"); let shm = PathBuf::from(shm);
    let has_wal = match std::fs::metadata(&wal) {
        Ok(meta) => meta.len() > 0,
        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => false,
        Err(e) => return Err(e.to_string()),
    };
    if has_wal && !shm.is_file() { return Err("store has a WAL without its shared-memory sidecar; run deploy on this stopped deployment to recover it before inspection".into()); }
    // Immutable SQLite omits WAL reads: use it only for the checkpointed main
    // image. Existing WAL+SHM can be read normally under the same exclusion.
    uri.push_str(if has_wal { "?mode=ro" } else { "?mode=ro&immutable=1" });
    let inspection = snapback4_core::store_port::AnyInspection::open_uri(&uri).map_err(|(_, e)|e)?;
    let release = inspection.optional_release().map_err(|(_, e)|e)?;
    let release = match release {
        Some(value) => value,
        None => std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("current")).map_err(|_| missing())?,
    };
    release.trim().parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing)
}

pub(super) fn bound_current(dir: &Path) -> Result<snapback4_lang::Bound, String> {
    let release = inspect_current(dir)?;
    bound_at(dir.join("releases").join(release.to_string()))
}

+ * Callback work must honor signal or bound its own external resources.
